   The entropy estimate answers the square-crossing question in [6, Section 11, Question 1]. Its
pivotal counterpart gives the other square-crossing bound in [11, Conjecture 8]. The singularity
theorem answers the discrete triangular-site case of [6, Section 11, Question 5]. Both questions
appeared in the 2008 preprint of [6], published in 2010. The spectral entropy bound is a percolation
instance of the Fourier entropy�influence conjecture of Friedgut and Kalai [4]; no assertion for
arbitrary Boolean functions is made here. Likewise, the singularity result concerns discrete laws. It
does not establish mutual singularity of their continuum limits or the square-lattice bond analogue.

1.3. The two uses of local irrelevance. The entropy upper bound encodes occupied cells through
successive spatial refinements. An occupied cell has one of fifteen nonempty four-child masks, so its
contribution is bounded by a constant times its occupancy probability. Four-arm estimates in the
interior and half-plane arm estimates near the boundary control the sum over cells. Positive relative
growth of m then sums the cost over scales. Pete proposed self-similarity and tree descriptions as a
route to the percolation entropy bounds in 2011 [15, slides 21�22]; the spectral local-mass estimate
in [6, Lemma 3.2 and equation (3.7)] is a related antecedent.


~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_30pp.txt:210

theorem.

1.3. The two uses of local irrelevance. The entropy upper bound encodes occupied cells through
successive spatial refinements. An occupied cell has one of fifteen nonempty four-child masks, so its
contribution is bounded by a constant times its occupancy probability. Four-arm estimates in the
interior and half-plane arm estimates near the boundary control the sum over cells. Positive relative
growth of m then sums the cost over scales. Pete proposed self-similarity and tree descriptions as a
route to the percolation entropy bounds in 2011 [15, slides 21�22]; the spectral local-mass estimate
in [6, Lemma 3.2 and equation (3.7)] is a related antecedent.

   For the entropy lower bound, a local configuration that makes a coordinate irrelevant contributes
zero to any Fourier coefficient containing that coordinate. Bounded overlap permits simultaneous
tests across a prescribed support and yields an exponential bound on each spectral atom. Closed
circuits give a block version of the argument, and hence the lower bound at each retained nonroot

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_30pp.txt:236
and a seven-color partition amplify the local estimate. The pivotal prohibition follows from a
path shortcut across a triangle. For geometric Fourier projections and conditional amplification in
percolation, see [6, Lemma 4.3 and Propositions 5.1 and 6.1].

   The local entropy estimate controls the masses of individual atoms; the wheel estimate controls
mass on a geometric class of supports. These are different conclusions from a common finite-product
method. The critical arm estimates then place both conclusions on the percolation scale. No
implication from entropy comparison to singularity is used.

   Fourier methods for noise sensitivity and their percolation applications originate in [1]; quantitative
developments include [16, 6, 19], with background in [7]. General entropy estimates and structured
Boolean classes are treated in [10, 9, 8, 14, 13, 2, 20, 5]. The spatial argument here removes the
logarithmic loss that coordinate subadditivity can retain.

1.4. Stability of the separation. The local statistic gives more than a separating event. Theo-
rem 11.1 shows that, under every coupling of the two laws, a fixed positive fraction of the spectral

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_30pp.txt:252
sites must be changed to obtain a pivotal realization, with probability tending to one. The minimum
expected number of changed sites is  m(R). If each spectral site is independently retained with
probability R, Proposition 11.2 proves that the face event still separates the thinned law from
every face-free law when 3Rm(R)  . When 3Rm(R)  0, that event has probability tending
to zero. Thus the face detector has retention threshold scale m(R)-1/3 = R-1/4+o(1) in these two
regimes. Corollary 11.3 records the nonempty testing error and the corresponding lower bound on
relative entropy.

   Sections 2�6 prove the entropy and spatial-resolution estimates. Sections 7 and 8 establish the
local separator and its finite-volume bounds. Section 9 proves the critical square rates, including
the sharp cost of low face density. Section 10 gives the abstract criterion and the product-measure
and quad extensions. Section 11 proves the perturbation and information consequences. Section 12
records the scope of the results and the remaining questions.

For triangular-lattice site percolation and square-lattice bond percolation, each guard uses at

most six coordinates. In the bond model the coordinate adjacency graph is the line graph of the

square lattice, of maximum degree six. A graph of maximum degree six has at most 1 + 6 + 6 � 5 = 37

vertices within distance two, so D = 6 and K = 37 are valid. In the geometric bond convention of

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_30pp.txt:506
centers whose neighborhoods intersect lie in a common closed neighborhood, so they have different

colors. The coloring alternative in Theorem 3.2 therefore applies with K = 7. The cancellation

in (17) is related to the restricted Fourier-coefficient argument in [6, Lemma 4.3]. Here bounded

overlap permits guards to be selected from an arbitrary Fourier support. Chang's lower bound for
the common-bias product measure �pn [3] uses a different influence transform and degenerates at
the unbiased parameter; the local guard hypothesis supplies a positive bound there.

                         11. Separation under perturbations

   The same argument applies to the spectral law conditioned to be nonempty in a fixed Jordan
quad, using Theorem 10.3. Its conclusion is the analogue of (55) for either the unconditional or the
nonempty-conditioned pivotal law. Thus local geometric separation cannot be removed by changing
a vanishing fraction of the spectral sites. This is a statement about the number of changed sites; it
does not assert a positive distance in a metric on continuum subsets.

                                      12. Scope and remaining questions

   The two questions in [6, Section 11] require different inputs. The entropy argument uses spatial
refinement, arm bounds, and local guards. The singularity argument additionally uses a local
pattern forbidden for pivotal sets and coercive for the spectrum. The main conclusions, including
their normalizations, can be summarized as follows.

                          ENTROPY AND SINGULARITY IN PERCOLATION                                29

Setting                   Entropy                       Geometric separation

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_30pp.txt:2183
region must have negligible spectral mass under the chosen normalization.

The arguments do not establish the unrestricted Fourier entropy�influence conjecture or the other

open questions in [6, Section 11].

Exact finite verification. The proof of Proposition 7.3 is analytic. For reproducibility, the source
archive also includes verification/verify_wheel.py, which reconstructs the integer Gram matrix,
verifies its characteristic polynomials and eigenvalue multiplicities in exact arithmetic, checks a
sharpness witness, and checks the seven-color disjoint-wheel partition. It uses Python and SymPy;
its saved output is included. No computation or external data is needed to compile the manuscript
or to apply the stated proofs.

   The entropy estimate answers the square-crossing question in [5, Section 11, Question 1]. Its
pivotal counterpart gives the other square-crossing bound in [9, Conjecture 8]. The singularity
theorem answers the discrete triangular-site case of [5, Section 11, Question 5]. Both questions
appeared in the 2008 preprint of [5], published in 2010. The spectral entropy bound is a percolation
instance of the Fourier entropy�influence conjecture of Friedgut and Kalai [4]; no assertion for
arbitrary Boolean functions is made here. Likewise, the singularity result concerns discrete laws. It
does not establish mutual singularity of their continuum limits or the square-lattice bond analogue.

   The conformal crossing limit gives R  0 [17]. No rate for the empty atom is asserted.
Theorem 10.3 extends discrete singularity to every fixed Jordan quad after conditioning the spectral
sample to be nonempty; the corresponding unconditioned conclusion retains the common empty
atom. Proposition 10.2 extends the finite face bounds to product measures with uniformly bounded
ring parameters. Those finite bounds alone do not supply a near-critical asymptotic singularity
theorem.

1.3. The two uses of local irrelevance. The entropy upper bound encodes occupied cells through
successive spatial refinements. An occupied cell has one of fifteen nonempty four-child masks, so
its contribution is bounded by a constant times its occupancy probability. Four-arm estimates in
the interior and half-plane arm estimates near the boundary control the sum over cells. Positive

   The conformal crossing limit gives R  0 [17]. No rate for the empty atom is asserted.
Theorem 10.3 extends discrete singularity to every fixed Jordan quad after conditioning the spectral
sample to be nonempty; the corresponding unconditioned conclusion retains the common empty
atom. Proposition 10.2 extends the finite face bounds to product measures with uniformly bounded
ring parameters. Those finite bounds alone do not supply a near-critical asymptotic singularity
theorem.

1.3. The two uses of local irrelevance. The entropy upper bound encodes occupied cells through
successive spatial refinements. An occupied cell has one of fifteen nonempty four-child masks, so
its contribution is bounded by a constant times its occupancy probability. Four-arm estimates in
the interior and half-plane arm estimates near the boundary control the sum over cells. Positive
relative growth of m then sums the cost over scales. In 2011 Pete asked whether self-similarity
forces the entropies of the pivotal set and the spectral sample to be at most a constant times their
expected sizes, with no logarithmic factor, and wrote that he thought he could do this for the
pivotal set but not for the spectral sample [15, slides 21�22]. The occupancy bound below starts

   For the entropy lower bound, a local configuration that makes a coordinate irrelevant contributes

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_31pp_other_version.txt:256
and a seven-color partition amplify the local estimate. The pivotal prohibition follows from a
path shortcut across a triangle. For geometric Fourier projections and conditional amplification in
percolation, see [5, Lemma 4.3 and Propositions 5.1 and 6.1].

   The local entropy estimate controls the masses of individual atoms; the wheel estimate controls
mass on a geometric class of supports. These are different conclusions from a common finite-product
method. The critical arm estimates then place both conclusions on the percolation scale. No
implication from entropy comparison to singularity is used.

      ENTROPY AND SINGULARITY IN PERCOLATION                                                   5

   Fourier methods for noise sensitivity and their percolation applications originate in [1]; quantitative
developments include [16, 5, 19], with background in [6]. General entropy estimates and structured
Boolean classes are treated in [10, 11, 8, 14, 13, 2, 20, 7]. The spatial argument here removes the
logarithmic loss that coordinate subadditivity can retain.


~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_31pp_other_version.txt:274
sites must be changed to obtain a pivotal realization, with probability tending to one. The minimum
expected number of changed sites is  m(R). If each spectral site is independently retained with
probability R, Proposition 11.2 proves that the face event still separates the thinned law from
every face-free law when 3Rm(R)  . When 3Rm(R)  0, that event has probability tending
to zero. Thus the face detector has retention threshold scale m(R)-1/3 = R-1/4+o(1) in these two
regimes. Corollary 11.3 records the nonempty testing error and the corresponding lower bound on
relative entropy.

   Sections 2�6 prove the entropy and spatial-resolution estimates. Sections 7 and 8 establish the
local separator and its finite-volume bounds. Section 9 proves the critical square rates, including
the sharp cost of low face density. Section 10 gives the abstract criterion and the product-measure
and quad extensions. Section 11 proves the perturbation and information consequences. Section 12
records the scope of the results and the remaining questions.

When the relative entropy is finite, Jensen's inequality under  gives KL()  -2 ln a; the same
lower bound is automatic when it is infinite. Hence

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_31pp_other_version.txt:2051
pointwise optimal test chooses the more likely atom; summing its error gives O/2.               

The face test alone has error of order R-1/2+o(1) under equal nonempty priors, by Theorem 1.5;

this matches the unconditional upper bound in (59). Under (10) the optimal test has the smaller

error  1  R-11/12+o(1);  no  efficient  implementation  of  that  optimal  test  is  asserted.
       2

                                 12. Scope and remaining questions

The two questions in [5, Section 11] require different inputs. The entropy argument uses spatial

refinement, arm bounds, and local guards. The singularity argument additionally uses a local

pattern forbidden for pivotal sets and coercive for the spectrum. The main conclusions, including

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_31pp_other_version.txt:2092
measures whose discrete total variation equals one can have identical weak limits. For example, 1/n
and -1/n converge to the same point mass. Thus the continuum alternative in GPS Question 5
remains a separate problem; Garban and Steif record the conjecture that the scaling limits of the

rescaled spectral sample and pivotal set are singular random compact sets [6, Chapter 10].

The thinning result identifies the transition scale for the face detector, but does not determine

whether the full laws remain separated below that scale. Likewise, the general-quad argument

provides qualitative separation after conditioning; it does not supply rates uniform over Jordan

boundaries. The near-critical finite coercivity bound isolates the additional inputs required for an

asymptotic extension: nonempty spectral size must escape bounded sets, and the untested boundary


~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\percolation_spectral_pivotal_entropy_31pp_other_version.txt:2108
region must have negligible spectral mass under the chosen normalization.

The arguments do not establish the unrestricted Fourier entropy�influence conjecture or the other

open questions in [5, Section 11].

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\threshold_paper_29pp_earlier_version.txt:208
   Shalev [20] studied the read-k DNF question and proved a min-entropy result for a restricted
class of such formulas. Arunachalam, Chakraborty, Kouck�, Saurabh and de Wolf [1] established the
min-entropy conjecture for read-k DNF with fixed k. Theorem C bounds the full Shannon entropy
and applies more generally to unions and intersections of regular functions. Fourier concentration
for small-read DNF was studied by Lecomte and Tan [13]; Section 8 records a direct concentration
consequence of the entropy bound.

   O'Donnell and Tan [16] introduced the product-measure strengthening of FEI used in composition
and proved its closure under disjoint substitution. They obtained FEI for read-once formulas over
any fixed finite gate basis. Our formula results use their composition principle; the additional input
is a bound uniform in the arity of symmetric threshold gates, together with the threshold and
regular-gate bounds above.

4                         YITZCHAK SHMALO

   Keller, Mossel and Schlank [12] obtained general entropy bounds involving the coordinate

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\threshold_paper_29pp_earlier_version.txt:230
entropy�influence comparison for crossing functions using spatial refinement and arm estimates, and
also controls the entropy of pivotal sets. The spectral crossing question was stated by Garban, Pete
and Schramm [8, Section 11]; Kalai [11, Conjecture 8] asked for both spectral and pivotal entropy
bounds.

1.2. The argument. The pivotal assignments of a regular function form an antichain on the
coordinates preceding the pivotal coordinate. Resampling part of that predecessor set therefore gives
a noise estimate relative to the mass of the derivative. In Fourier space, this bounds the expected
logarithmic discrepancy between a coordinate's rank and the number of predecessors present in a
spectral set.

   A reference distribution on sets of a fixed size converts that rank bound into entropy. Several
regular functions are handled by partitioning each pivotal set among its component functions and
using the least expensive of their coordinate orders. To obtain the bound by D, we multiply by the
character that minimizes influence. This changes signs, but preserves the antichain supports of all
derivative pieces outside the selected character. The remaining coordinates receive zero rank cost.

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\threshold_paper_29pp_earlier_version.txt:287
This follows by normalizing Q and applying nonnegativity of relative entropy.

4.3. Several desirability chains. A desirability chain partition is a partition of the coordinates
into ordered lists such that every earlier coordinate in a list is at least as desirable as every later
one. The orientation is fixed for the whole function. Singleton lists always give such a partition.

   The classes in Theorems A, B, and 4.2 are closed under coordinate restrictions. As another
consequence, if fixing k specified coordinates makes every restriction regular, the function is a
Boolean combination of those k coordinate functions and its 2k regular restrictions. Theorem B
gives a constant of order k + 1 for this class.

                              5. Read-k unions of structured gates

   A union can contain arbitrarily many component functions while each input is relevant to only a
few of them. We show that this overlap parameter controls Fourier entropy. In this section a union is
true, with value 1, when at least one component has value 1. Intersections follow by complementing
the components and the output.

of at most M regular functions, and every input coordinate is relevant to at most k of the functions

consequence, if fixing k specified coordinates makes every restriction regular, the function is a
Boolean combination of those k coordinate functions and its 2k regular restrictions. Theorem B
gives a constant of order k + 1 for this class.

                              5. Read-k unions of structured gates

   A union can contain arbitrarily many component functions while each input is relevant to only a
few of them. We show that this overlap parameter controls Fourier entropy. In this section a union is
true, with value 1, when at least one component has value 1. Intersections follow by complementing
the components and the output.

    8. Further consequences and questions

The scalar inequality r  1 + log r on [1, 2] follows by concavity. Theorem C with M = 1 now gives

H(f )  2(41 + 7 log k) log(2s).

If H(f ) = 0, one coefficient carries all the mass. Otherwise apply Markov's inequality to log(1/f (S)2)

under the squared Fourier law. The sets with f (S)2  2-H(f)/ carry mass at least 1 - , and their

number is at most 2H(f)/. Parseval gives the approximation assertion.  

8.2. The product-measure question for weighted thresholds. The next composition question
is whether an absolute constant C satisfies

                                              H�+(f )  C I�(f ) - Var�(f )

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\threshold_paper_29pp_earlier_version.txt:1989
                                              H�+(f )  C I�(f ) - Var�(f )

for every weighted linear threshold function and every product measure. Theorem 6.6 proves this for
symmetric thresholds. A positive answer for arbitrary weights would remove the balance assumption
on weighted internal gates in Corollary 7.4, by the same exact composition identities.

   The antichain argument raises a second question. Can a spectral reference measure control the
cost of many incomparable coordinate orders without a factor depending on their number? The
width bound of Theorem 4.2 and the overlap bound of Theorem C provide two ways to limit that
cost. The unrestricted FEI conjecture requires an argument without either restriction.

   A Boolean function is unate if it becomes monotone after reversing some input coordinates. For
a monotone function, coordinate j is at least as desirable as coordinate i if

(4)                         f (xj=1,i=-1)  f (xj=-1,i=1)

for every assignment to the other coordinates. We call a unate function regular if, in a monotone
orientation, its coordinates admit an order in which every earlier coordinate is at least as desirable

   The joint witness estimate in Proposition 5.5 retains the cost of the gates used by an entire
Fourier set and gives a constant bound for addressing inside the same queue construction. Section 8
derives exact selector identities and constant-preserving closure under suitable Fourier-support
separation. It also shows that unrestricted two-cell selectors and arbitrarily rare activations preserve
the optimal constant of full FEI. These reductions specify the scope of the structured hypotheses.

1.1. Earlier work. O'Donnell, Wright and Zhou [18] proved FEI for symmetric and fixed-part
symmetric functions and for read-once decision trees. Their method relates derivative noise
stability to spectral entropy; the spectral-level argument below refines their bound. Chakraborty,
Kulkarni, Lokam and Saurabh [5] proved, among other results, an O( n) entropy bound for linear
threshold functions. The corrected version of their manuscript removes an earlier all-threshold claim.
Chakraborty, Karmalkar, Kundu, Lokam and Saurabh [4] proved FEI with high probability for
specified random models of linear threshold functions.


~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\threshold_paper_38pp.txt:209
   Shalev [21] studied the read-k DNF question and proved a min-entropy result for a restricted
class of such formulas. Arunachalam, Chakraborty, Kouck�, Saurabh and de Wolf [1] established the
min-entropy conjecture for read-k DNF with fixed k. Theorem C bounds the full Shannon entropy
and applies more generally to unions and intersections of regular functions. Fourier concentration
for small-read DNF was studied by Lecomte and Tan [14]; Section 9 records a direct concentration
consequence of the entropy bound.

4                         YITZCHAK SHMALO

   O'Donnell and Tan [17] introduced the product-measure strengthening of FEI used in composition
and proved its closure under disjoint substitution. They obtained FEI for read-once formulas over
any fixed finite gate basis. Our formula results use their composition principle; the additional input
is a bound uniform in the arity of symmetric threshold gates, together with the threshold and
regular-gate bounds above.

   Keller, Mossel and Schlank [13] obtained general entropy bounds involving the coordinate

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\threshold_paper_38pp.txt:231
entropy�influence comparison for crossing functions using spatial refinement and arm estimates, and
also controls the entropy of pivotal sets. The spectral crossing question was stated by Garban, Pete
and Schramm [9, Section 11]; Kalai [12, Conjecture 8] asked for both spectral and pivotal entropy
bounds.

1.2. The argument. The pivotal assignments of a regular function form an antichain on the
coordinates preceding the pivotal coordinate. Resampling part of that predecessor set therefore gives
a noise estimate relative to the mass of the derivative. In Fourier space, this bounds the expected
logarithmic discrepancy between a coordinate's rank and the number of predecessors present in a
spectral set.

   A reference distribution on sets of a fixed size converts that rank bound into entropy. Several
regular functions are handled by partitioning each pivotal set among its component functions and
using the least expensive of their coordinate orders. To obtain the bound by D, we multiply by the
character that minimizes influence. This changes signs, but preserves the antichain supports of all
derivative pieces outside the selected character. The remaining coordinates receive zero rank cost.

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\threshold_paper_38pp.txt:295

4.4. Several desirability chains. A desirability chain partition is a partition of the coordinates
into ordered lists such that every earlier coordinate in a list is at least as desirable as every later
one. The orientation is fixed for the whole function. Singleton lists always give such a partition.

Theorem 4.2. Let w  1. If a unate Boolean function f has a desirability chain partition with at

   The classes in Theorems A, B, and 4.2 are closed under coordinate restrictions. As another
consequence, if fixing k specified coordinates makes every restriction regular, the function is a
Boolean combination of those k coordinate functions and its 2k regular restrictions. Theorem B
gives a constant of order k + 1 for this class.

                              5. Read-k unions of structured gates

   A union can contain arbitrarily many component functions while each input is relevant to only a
few of them. We show that this overlap parameter controls Fourier entropy. In this section a union is
true, with value 1, when at least one component has value 1. Intersections follow by complementing
the components and the output.

Proof. Theorem A gives the constant Creg at every regular leaf. Extending a branch by irrelevant

data coordinates changes neither its entropy nor its influence. Thus the branches at each node can

Creg > 4. Induction proves the claim.

   Thus a uniform estimate for arbitrary sparsely activated cores would resolve full FEI. Rarity and
small total influence alone impose no smaller optimal constant. This conclusion concerns arbitrary
cores; the structured gate hypotheses in the preceding theorems supply additional information.

       9. Further consequences and questions

9.1. Spectral concentration for read-k DNF. Entropy bounds give sparse Fourier approxima-
tions directly. We record the consequence for DNF to make the dependence on the approximation
error explicit. This is the concentration problem studied by Mansour [15]; sharper sparsity bounds
as a function of the read number are known from Lecomte and Tan [14].

Corollary 9.1. Let f have a read-k DNF representation with s  1 terms. For every 0 <  < 1,
there is a family A of Fourier sets such that

           f (S)2  1 - ,                       |A|  (2s) 2(41+7 log k)/.


~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\threshold_paper_38pp.txt:2691
                                              t1

The scalar inequality r  1 + log r on [1, 2] follows by concavity. Theorem C with M = 1 now gives

    H(f )  2(41 + 7 log k) log(2s).

If H(f ) = 0, one coefficient carries all the mass. Otherwise apply Markov's inequality to log(1/f (S)2)

under the squared Fourier law. The sets with f (S)2  2-H(f)/ carry mass at least 1 - , and their

number is at most 2H(f)/. Parseval gives the approximation assertion.  

9.2. The product-measure question for weighted thresholds. The next composition question
is whether an absolute constant C satisfies

                                              H+� (f )  C I�(f ) - Var�(f )

~\brain_workers\FEI100_20261011\context\threshold_paper_38pp.txt:2706
                                              H+� (f )  C I�(f ) - Var�(f )

for every weighted linear threshold function and every product measure. Theorem 6.6 proves this for
symmetric thresholds. A positive answer for arbitrary weights would remove the balance assumption
on weighted internal gates in Corollary 7.4, by the same exact composition identities.

   The antichain argument raises a second question. Can a spectral reference measure control the
cost of many incomparable coordinate orders without a factor depending on their number? The
width bound of Theorem 4.2 and the overlap bound of Theorem C provide two ways to limit that
cost. The unrestricted FEI conjecture requires an argument without either restriction.

{\Large\bfseries\color{ink} A 100-agent research plan for the full\\[3pt] Fourier entropy--influence conjecture}\par

Asia/Jerusalem (UTC+3 throughout this interval)
\end{center}
\vspace{6pt}
\textbf{Purpose.} Concentrate a hypothetical capacity of 100 Codex research agents on the missing mathematical estimates needed for unrestricted FEI, using the recovered threshold paper, the separate reductions manuscript, and the existing Brain obstruction archive. The same twenty research streams are assigned to the twenty existing scheduled tasks in this environment. This report is a private research plan; it is not part of the public paper.

\textbf{Current mathematical status.} The recovered preprint proves structured FEI results, led by an all-threshold theorem with constant below $8.937$. The joint witness construction and exact selector identities are valid tools. A universal bound for their remaining entropy cost is unproved. Neither this report nor the computations establish full FEI. A hundred agents can substantially expand the search and review capacity; the number of agents does not supply the missing inequality or guarantee a solution within ten days.

\textbf{Recommendation.} Spend the largest share of proof effort on the \emph{posterior cost of an entire Fourier-set witness} and on \emph{nonlocal restriction accounting}. Keep a substantial independent effort on signed Booleanity and product-biased gate inequalities. Use targeted falsification continuously, particularly on addressing, rare activation, near-tensor surgery, and actual Boolean realizations of proposed spectral laws. Treat every surviving universal reformulation as an unpaid obligation until its quantitative bound is proved.

\newpage
\section{The exact target and the starting assets}

For $f:\bits^n\to\bits$ under the uniform measure, set
\[
 P_f(S)=\wh f(S)^2,\qquad
 \HH(f)=\sum_S P_f(S)\log_2\frac1{P_f(S)},\qquad
 \I(f)=\sum_S |S|P_f(S).
\]

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:54
 \qquad \HH(f)\le C\I(f).
\end{equation}
The constant must be independent of dimension, arity, depth, overlap, probability of the minority output, and any unbounded representation parameter. An explicit large constant would settle the conjecture. Optimizing that constant is secondary to proving its finiteness.

Throughout this report, entropy is in bits. Some historical Brain notes use natural logarithms and raw entropy of indicators. A coefficient in such a note cannot be copied into a sign-function statement without the corresponding conversion. Every imported result must state its measure, Fourier basis, normalization, log base, and treatment of the empty coefficient.

\subsection{Recovered manuscripts and verified scope}

The interrupted work supplied a 36-page version of \emph{Fourier entropy of threshold functions and their compositions}. The audited recovery and shared-data extension produce the 38-page public paper, released at repository commit \texttt{cd27aa96a5bb670a5b3ae5451095b7020db8e9d4} \cite{Threshold}. Its principal constant is
\[
 \Creg=\log_2(4+e)+2\log_2(e\pi)
       =8.93647465787758\ldots.
\]
With $D(f)=\sum_i\min\{\I_i(f),1-\I_i(f)\}$, its Theorem A gives $\HH(f)\le\Creg D(f)$ for every regular Boolean function. Here regular means unate with a total desirability order, and therefore includes every deterministic linear threshold function with arbitrary real weights. It does not mean small or uniformly comparable weights.

The proof proceeds through pivotal antichains, a relative resampling estimate, harmonic control of spectral ranks, and an Euler-product reference on Fourier levels. The proof was reconstructed in an independent audit during recovery; the product-biased threshold and bounded-overlap arguments were also independently read. No gap was identified in those audits. These are mathematical checks of a preprint, not external peer review or proof-assistant certification.

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:65
       =8.93647465787758\ldots.
\]
With $D(f)=\sum_i\min\{\I_i(f),1-\I_i(f)\}$, its Theorem A gives $\HH(f)\le\Creg D(f)$ for every regular Boolean function. Here regular means unate with a total desirability order, and therefore includes every deterministic linear threshold function with arbitrary real weights. It does not mean small or uniformly comparable weights.

The proof proceeds through pivotal antichains, a relative resampling estimate, harmonic control of spectral ranks, and an Euler-product reference on Fourier levels. The proof was reconstructed in an independent audit during recovery; the product-biased threshold and bounded-overlap arguments were also independently read. No gap was identified in those audits. These are mathematical checks of a preprint, not external peer review or proof-assistant certification.

The package also contains logarithmic FEI bounds for Boolean combinations of regular functions and for read-$k$ unions of structured gates, and an arity-independent corrected FEI+ estimate for symmetric thresholds under every product measure. The latter composes through read-once threshold/parity formulas. The proof of a product-biased estimate for \emph{arbitrary weighted thresholds} is not supplied.

The public recovery revision includes the exact shared-data multiplexer identity from the separate reductions manuscript, with the improved regular-leaf constant. If adjacent branches are orthogonal and selectors are fresh at each node, recursive multiplexers preserve $\Creg$ even when the branch data variables overlap. This is a concrete further class theorem, with explicit hypotheses.

The second recovered manuscript, \emph{Restrictions and witness coding for the Fourier entropy--influence problem} \cite{Reductions}, contains conditioned posterior forests, exact physical-restriction identities, and simultaneous hardness-preserving transformations. It supplies research targets and counterexamples for this plan. It is not silently substituted for the threshold paper or published as part of the report.

\subsection{Why the literature check matters}

The corrected Chakraborty--Kulkarni--Lokam--Saurabh work bounds LTF entropy by $O(\sqrt n)$; an earlier all-LTF claim was removed. The random-LTF result treats specified random models \cite{CKLS,RandomLTF}. This makes verification of the recovered deterministic theorem particularly important. The current argument must stand on its complete proof, independently of earlier abstracts or claims.


~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:71
The package also contains logarithmic FEI bounds for Boolean combinations of regular functions and for read-$k$ unions of structured gates, and an arity-independent corrected FEI+ estimate for symmetric thresholds under every product measure. The latter composes through read-once threshold/parity formulas. The proof of a product-biased estimate for \emph{arbitrary weighted thresholds} is not supplied.

The public recovery revision includes the exact shared-data multiplexer identity from the separate reductions manuscript, with the improved regular-leaf constant. If adjacent branches are orthogonal and selectors are fresh at each node, recursive multiplexers preserve $\Creg$ even when the branch data variables overlap. This is a concrete further class theorem, with explicit hypotheses.

The second recovered manuscript, \emph{Restrictions and witness coding for the Fourier entropy--influence problem} \cite{Reductions}, contains conditioned posterior forests, exact physical-restriction identities, and simultaneous hardness-preserving transformations. It supplies research targets and counterexamples for this plan. It is not silently substituted for the threshold paper or published as part of the report.

\subsection{Why the literature check matters}

The corrected Chakraborty--Kulkarni--Lokam--Saurabh work bounds LTF entropy by $O(\sqrt n)$; an earlier all-LTF claim was removed. The random-LTF result treats specified random models \cite{CKLS,RandomLTF}. This makes verification of the recovered deterministic theorem particularly important. The current argument must stand on its complete proof, independently of earlier abstracts or claims.

Existing coding and composition theorems are essential prerequisites, especially Wan--Wright--Wu and O'Donnell--Tan \cite{WWW,ODT}. Shalev's low-influence reductions explain why rarity alone is not a structural simplification \cite{Shalev}. Recent restriction work supplies a directly relevant stopping-tree framework \cite{GMP}; the plateaued-function theorem supplies a useful structured benchmark \cite{Potapov}. The author's percolation result supplies a geometric encoding example, with spatial hypotheses that must remain explicit \cite{Percolation}.

Do not spend the campaign rediscovering the generic coordinate-marginal entropy estimate as a Boolean breakthrough. Keller--Mossel--Schlank already give the relevant general estimate \cite{KMS}. A later preprint claiming an information-theoretic sharpening was withdrawn because the result already appeared there \cite{Withdrawn}. A new universal argument must use additional structure of actual Boolean spectra or a genuinely stronger global accounting mechanism.

\section{The two highest-priority mathematical endpoints}

\subsection{Endpoint J: bound a complete witness, including its posterior entropy}

For a nonconstant Boolean function represented as a finite union of nonconstant gates with finite ordered covers of their relevant supports, the recovered queue construction generates a subprobability law on Fourier sets through finite successful runs. A partition forest assigns every coordinate of a nonempty set $A$ to a gate, visits each used gate once, and describes gate subsets, roots, attachments, and child labels. Its probability is an actual probability of the fixed random generator; failed or infinite runs contribute no mass. A Fourier-set-dependent choice of a \emph{witness} is legitimate because the reference generator itself remains fixed.

The conditioned version chooses global positive weights $\lambda_l$ satisfying
\[
 \sum_{l:j\in R_l}\lambda_l\le1\quad\text{for every coordinate }j.
\]

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:156
 \I(f)=\I_i(f)+\tfrac12\bigl[\I(f_i^+)+\I(f_i^-)\bigr].
\]
Thus a theorem asserting that every nonconstant Boolean $f$ has a relevant coordinate with $B_i(f)\le K\I_i(f)$ would settle FEI with constant $K+2/\ln2$. Conversely, a global FEI constant $C$ implies existence of such a coordinate with $K=C$, because $\sum_i B_i(f)\le\HH(f)$. This is an exact existential route to finiteness, applied at every reached restricted function; it does not assert the inequality for every coordinate. A restricted-class version requires the corresponding hereditary hypothesis or an explicitly paid stopping rule.

The scalar decrement bound behaves as
\[
 \kappa(u)=u\log_2(1/u)+\frac{3u}{\ln2}+O(u^2)
 \quad(u\downarrow0).
\]
Consequently a local influence budget can lose a logarithm. The task is to select a good coordinate, use a whole block, or amortize difficult splits against later credit. A finite restriction tree satisfies
\begin{align*}
 \HH(f)&=\E\sum_{v\text{ visited}}\Delta_{i(v)}\HH(f_v)
                +\E\HH(f_{\rm leaf}),\\
 \I(f)&=\E\sum_{v\text{ visited}}\I_{i(v)}(f_v)
                +\E\I(f_{\rm leaf}).
\end{align*}

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:170
                +\E\I(f_{\rm leaf}).
\end{align*}
A successful stopping-tree theorem must bound the \emph{entire expected path cost} and the terminal entropy. The recovered threshold and formula classes provide useful terminal classes. The structural theorem guaranteeing cheap access to those terminals remains to be proved.


For a fixed block $W$, the recovered block identity is
\begin{align}
 \Delta_W\HH(f)
 &:=
 \HH(f)-\E_y\HH(f|_{W=y})\notag\\
 &=H(\mathcal S\cap W\mid\mathcal S\cap W^c)+M_W(f),
 \label{eq:block-erasure}\\
 0&\le M_W(f)\le\frac2{\ln2}\sum_{i\in W}\I_i(f),\notag\\
 \I(f)-\E_y\I(f|_{W=y})&=\sum_{i\in W}\I_i(f).\label{eq:block-influence}
\end{align}
The exposure is uniform physical restriction. An adaptive block rule

\cite{B05Rare}; the displayed no-go has been independently
checked analytically. Its floating-point self-check is
supporting computation, not the proof of the asymptotic theorem.

\subsection{Boolean phase and conditional entropy}

For an actual Boolean sign, $f^2=1$ yields the signed convolution identities
\[
 \sum_S\wh f(S)\wh f(S\mathbin\triangle U)=1_{\{U=\varnothing\}}.
\]
An arbitrary probability law on subsets need not admit signs satisfying these identities. The Brain's cyclic-label construction disproves a proposed dimension-free signed-lifetime bound for an auxiliary subset law, but that divergent law is provably non-Boolean \cite{Brain51}. It is a powerful warning against overbroad relaxations, not an FEI counterexample.

\section{Ten-day sequence, checkpoints, and reallocation}

The campaign ends at 22:15:57 Asia/Jerusalem on 21 October 2026. The twenty recurrences are bounded to that window; the final scheduled occurrence for a particular slot may precede the cutoff because its hourly phase is preserved. The prompt also carries the explicit deadline.

\begin{longtable}{>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.16\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.40\linewidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.36\linewidth}}
\toprule
Window & Work to complete & Evidence required to advance \\
\midrule\endhead
Days 1--2 & Recover and freeze statements; reproduce decisive old controls; create exact J/R and Booleanity target ledgers; divide nonoverlapping packets. & Source-pinned baseline, no known falsifier omitted, precise first unpaid lemma for every live route. \\
Days 3--4 & Prove restricted quantitative endpoints, construct candidate codes or stopping rules, and attack them with targeted searches. & At least one fully written new lemma or a rigorous route-killing family per productive stream. \\

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:1161
Days 3--4 & Prove restricted quantitative endpoints, construct candidate codes or stopping rules, and attack them with targeted searches. & At least one fully written new lemma or a rigorous route-killing family per productive stream. \\
Day 5 & Compare the live routes and reallocate effort. Attempt cross-route transfer into an exact missing term. & Keep routes with a quantitative advance or a sharply surviving bottleneck; archive broad reformulations with no new bound. \\
Days 6--8 & Concentrate proof effort on the strongest two to four surviving mechanisms; complete a substantial class theorem when possible. & Explicit constants, no unproved representation theorem hidden in a lemma, and complete treatment of difficult endpoints. \\
Day 9 & Complete integrated independent reconstruction of full-FEI candidates and major restricted theorems after rolling reviews throughout the campaign; run only computations needed by the proof. & Two substantive independent audits, exact dependency graph, all counterexample objections resolved or the theorem narrowed. \\
Day 10 & Complete accepted proofs, document rejected routes, and deliver the exact remaining frontier. & A self-contained final manuscript or verified partial-result package; complete private archives and concrete continuation packets. \\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

Independent review starts immediately on Day 1; Day 9 is an integrated recheck of candidates and dependencies, not a first review. The end-of-day checkpoints are 13, 15, 16, 19, 20 and 21 October 2026 at 22:15:57 Asia/Jerusalem for Days 2, 4, 5, 8, 9 and 10 respectively. The synthesis stream should check progress at least at each natural invocation, but numerical quotas are not success criteria. If a difficult lemma needs several cycles, record its exact latest derivation and unresolved line. Repetition becomes useful only when a new idea, a stronger hypothesis, a different proof, or a new falsifier is introduced.

\subsection{Rules for stopping an unproductive route}

\begin{enumerate}
\item If a known Boolean counterexample violates the proposed helper, stop using that helper immediately. Retain the failed statement and its certificate; attempt a repair only with a stated new hypothesis.
\item If an arbitrary Boolean seed embeds in a proposed simplified class while preserving the target ratio, label the endpoint FEI-equivalent. It can still be pursued, but it is not counted as a reduction in difficulty without a new estimate.
\item If a proof works for arbitrary spectral probability laws but such laws have a known counterexample, locate the missing Boolean constraint before continuing.

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:1162

Days 6--8 & Concentrate proof effort on the strongest two to four surviving mechanisms; complete a substantial class theorem when possible. & Explicit constants, no unproved representation theorem hidden in a lemma, and complete treatment of difficult endpoints. \\
Day 9 & Complete integrated independent reconstruction of full-FEI candidates and major restricted theorems after rolling reviews throughout the campaign; run only computations needed by the proof. & Two substantive independent audits, exact dependency graph, all counterexample objections resolved or the theorem narrowed. \\
Day 10 & Complete accepted proofs, document rejected routes, and deliver the exact remaining frontier. & A self-contained final manuscript or verified partial-result package; complete private archives and concrete continuation packets. \\
\bottomrule

\item If an arbitrary Boolean seed embeds in a proposed simplified class while preserving the target ratio, label the endpoint FEI-equivalent. It can still be pursued, but it is not counted as a reduction in difficulty without a new estimate.
\item If a proof works for arbitrary spectral probability laws but such laws have a known counterexample, locate the missing Boolean constraint before continuing.
\item If successive cycles only rename the same residual or add another equivalent potential, redirect some capacity to a concrete restricted theorem or a different mechanism.
\item If a finite computation is the only evidence for a universal statement, require a finite-to-infinite theorem or lower the claim to the exact tested domain.
\end{enumerate}

These are scientific allocation rules. They do not authorize overriding access restrictions, manufacturing a tool result, or abandoning a scheduled slot. A slot with a blocked action should continue independent permitted mathematical work and preserve the actual blocker.

\section{What would count as a significant result}

\subsection{Primary outcome: the unrestricted theorem or a rigorous disproof}

A full proof must close \eqref{eq:full} for every Boolean function and every dimension with one finite constant. It must contain its representation, selection, coding, or structural lemmas as proved statements, including any existence argument. A disproof must construct actual Boolean functions $f_m$ with $H(f_m)/I(f_m)\to\infty$. Exceeding one proposed constant or falsifying an auxiliary spectral inequality does not meet that standard.

A universal good-coordinate erasure inequality, a universal
whole-witness residual bound, or a globally summable stopping

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:1176

\item If a finite computation is the only evidence for a universal statement, require a finite-to-infinite theorem or lower the claim to the exact tested domain.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Primary outcome: the unrestricted theorem or a rigorous disproof}

A full proof must close \eqref{eq:full} for every Boolean function and every dimension with one finite constant. It must contain its representation, selection, coding, or structural lemmas as proved statements, including any existence argument. whole-witness residual bound, or a globally summable stopping
tree with uniformly paid terminals would close a precise route
to \eqref{eq:full}. These are full-solution outcomes. Their
restricted-class variants can be substantial partial results.

\subsection{Major partial outcomes worth completing}

\begin{itemize}
\item A good-coordinate or fully paid stopping-tree theorem for a new explicit class, with its hereditary or admission hypothesis proved and the remaining universal obstruction identified.

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:1196

\begin{itemize}
\item A good-coordinate or fully paid stopping-tree theorem for a new explicit class, with its hereditary or admission hypothesis proved and the remaining universal obstruction identified.
\item A bound on the whole-witness residual for a class with genuinely unbounded overlap that lies outside the existing bounded-read or orthogonal-multiplexer hypotheses. State exactly how the new parameter improves the old logarithmic loss.
\item An arity-uniform product-biased corrected FEI+ theorem for arbitrary weighted thresholds, with its resulting depth-independent read-once formula consequence.
\item A Boolean-specific conditional entropy or phase-energy theorem under a proved stable structural condition, together with a clear account of the remaining class outside that condition.
\item A full FEI theorem for another broad, currently unresolved family, supported by a primary-source novelty check. Do not assert that a family is new merely because it is absent from one local manuscript.
\item A rigorous scalable counterfamily that rules out a major live universal helper, or a corrected sharp auxiliary theorem replacing it. Such a result can save substantial research effort without being a disproof of FEI.
\end{itemize}

Improving $8.937$ to a slightly smaller number, running more random examples of an already proved identity, or producing longer exposition without a new mathematical implication is a lower priority during this campaign. These can be useful after a major theorem is secure, but they should not displace work on the unpaid global terms.

\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Target:} exact statement, units, domain, hypotheses, and intended implication for FEI.
\item \textbf{Inputs:} source files actually read, immutable revisions, theorem/page locators, and the exact prior open step.
\item \textbf{Result:} full proof, counterexample, or explicitly incomplete derivation. State the first remaining unsupported line.
\item \textbf{Validation:} original commands, code, deterministic seeds where used, exit status, tested domain, error bounds, and actual outputs. Mark unexecuted work as such.
\item \textbf{Dependencies:} accepted lemmas, unverified lemmas, falsified candidates, and whether this result changes any upstream conclusion.
\item \textbf{Handoff:} one next mathematical action with its expected deliverable and a distinct proposed reviewer.
\item \textbf{Persistence:} actual repository commit and paths, plus any unresolved save, delivery, or access failure.
\end{enumerate}

\subsection{Acceptance states}

Use distinct states for a proposed claim, an analytically proved claim, a finite verified statement, an independently reviewed theorem, and a public release. A finite computational check can verify a finite statement exactly; it cannot by itself promote an all-dimensional theorem. A source index or repository upload does not imply native Brain ingestion or downstream use.

For a complete full-FEI candidate, both audit streams must inspect the complete dependency chain and the known obstruction suite. They should independently reconstruct the critical inequalities and check all limit passages. A proof-assistant certificate is valuable when available, but absence of one is not replaced by assertions of formal verification. The author decides dissemination of future results; the present authorization publishes the recovered paper and keeps this plan and campaign research in the requested private repositories.

\subsection{How the twenty scheduled streams execute the hundred assignments}

The machine-readable assignment file is an execution contract: every A-packet has a work type, priority, expected artifacts, specific acceptance criteria, a bounded initial pilot, suggested upstream packets, intended consumers, and independent reviewers. Suggested upstream packets are research connections, not proved premises. An actual proof must list the exact claim versions it uses; that proof-dependency graph must be acyclic even when the research workflow has feedback loops.

Each natural invocation of B01--B20 selects \emph{one} of its five A-packets. It resumes a productive unfinished derivation, consumes a useful new dependency, or starts the nearest ready obligation. It does not rotate mechanically through five topics or start five additional workers. If access or a missing input blocks that packet, it works on an independent ready packet within the same FEI stream. Assistance comes through source-bound handoffs to existing streams. The existing twenty task identities, hourly phases, recurrence counts and fixed endpoint remain unchanged.

Every run records its A-packet, stable gap identifier, attempt identifier, method, exact latest derivation, and next action. Three substantive attempts on the same unresolved gap without a new quantitative bound, structural distinction, valid counterexample, or repaired proof trigger a short diagnosis and a change of method or a narrower theorem. Renaming a potential does not reset that count. An access failure or a bookkeeping-only run is recorded honestly and is not counted as a mathematical attempt.

Baseline work has a finite completion event. Once a source-complete identity, convention ledger, calibration fixture, or dependency representation is accepted, later runs consume it and move to a downstream obligation. Reopening requires a concrete correction or new application. The recovered manuscript and completed audits count toward the first checkpoint; this revision does not restart two days of recovery.


~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:1251

\subsection{Versioned completion and refutation propagation}

The claim registry also holds stable gap identifiers. Their canonical live versions are in the private open-problems campaign directory, with a Brain mirror. B20 maintains these concise indices from original run artifacts; workers save uniquely named runs instead of all overwriting the same state file.

The progress record names the packet, gap, attempt, mathematical change, proof dependencies, result sources, next consumers, and next action. A claim carries an exact statement and hypothesis hash, version, proof source, contributor list, review records and any supersedes or refutes relationship. A completed artifact, a proved mathematical target, an independent review, a saved mirror and downstream use are separate states. A final honest gap report can complete a handoff while leaving the mathematical target open.

A completed artifact, a proved mathematical target, an independent review, a saved mirror and downstream use are separate states. A final honest gap report can complete a handoff while leaving the mathematical target open.

When a claim version is refuted, B20 flags its dependent conclusions as \texttt{BLOCKED\_DEPENDENCY} or \texttt{CONDITIONAL}, preserves the original witness, and routes the correction to the named consumers. A consumer records the actual repaired substitution in its next substantive report. Delivery receipts are not evidence that a correction has been used in a proof. The registry organizes evidence; it cannot certify a theorem by changing a status field.

At each invocation B20 checks coverage of all hundred A-packets and all report elements: exact endpoints, representation assumptions, normalization, mandatory counterexamples, computations, reviews, class consequences and final handoff. It identifies unattended high-priority work and assigns the nearest ready step within existing capacity. Its own run must also contain a substantive synthesis or derivation. The aim is to finish every useful deliverable while retaining an accurate list of mathematically unresolved targets.

\subsection{Review starts immediately}

Independent review runs from Day 1. Day 9 is an integrated recheck of the strongest candidates and their repaired dependencies, not the first reading of individual proofs. A stream may hold at most two complete candidate claims awaiting review; when that queue is full, it completes a derivation, resolves an objection, verifies a witness or reviews an independent claim before opening another broad conjecture. Exploratory inferences may continue with explicit conditional status; two complete audits are reserved for full-FEI candidates and major theorems selected for acceptance.

A reviewer who supplies a substantive missing proof step becomes a contributor to that version and cannot serve as its independent accepting reviewer. Re-running the author's code or reading the same summary is not an independent reconstruction. A different representation, proof route or source-level reconstruction is required for the critical claim.

\subsection{Bounded computation and escalation}

Before a search, freeze the helper version, actual Boolean family, parameter domain, case count, justified symmetries, arithmetic requirements, memory estimate and maximum runtime. Run an initial pilot of at most sixty seconds to measure throughput; this is a cap for the pilot, not a grant of additional computing resources. Search structured families cheaply before attempting exhaustive tables. The number of Boolean truth tables is $2^{2^n}$: $65{,}536$ for $n=4$, $4{,}294{,}967{,}296$ for $n=5$, and $18{,}446{,}744{,}073{,}709{,}551{,}616$ for $n=6$. Do not increase dimension automatically after a negative search.

\subsection{Bounded computation and escalation}

Before a search, freeze the helper version, actual Boolean family, parameter domain, case count, justified symmetries, arithmetic requirements, memory estimate and maximum runtime. Run an initial pilot of at most sixty seconds to measure throughput; this is a cap for the pilot, not a grant of additional computing resources. Search structured families cheaply before attempting exhaustive tables. The number of Boolean truth tables is $2^{2^n}$: $65{,}536$ for $n=4$, $4{,}294{,}967{,}296$ for $n=5$, and $18{,}446{,}744{,}073{,}709{,}551{,}616$ for $n=6$. Do not increase dimension automatically after a negative search.

Use floating point for candidate discovery, then exact integer Walsh transforms or explicit analytic spectra to establish Boolean realizability, Parseval and influence. Certify a decisive logarithmic sign through rigorous intervals, an integer-product comparison, or analysis; stop increasing precision once the sign is settled. Independently reconstruct a decisive witness using a different implementation or representation. A finite-to-infinite, all-parameter or continuum theorem is a separate obligation. Record observed runtime and outputs without inventing token prices, continuous availability or unobserved execution.

\subsection{Reconciliation with the concurrent mathematical supplement}

The source-pinned supplement at \texttt{research\_pushes/2026-10-11-full-fei-100-agents/} in both private repositories contributes useful mathematics to this same campaign. Its \texttt{NATIVE\_MAPPING.md} explicitly maps advisory F-role groups to the installed B-streams. The F labels do not create another hundred workers or replace the A-packet backlog. Its report, counterexample archive and seed lemmas are consulted for a concrete obligation within the existing assignment.

In particular, \texttt{SEED\_LEMMAS.md} already derives a normalized prior mixture of complete reference laws, an effective-piece rank refinement under explicit disjoint-support and fiber-antichain hypotheses, and a point-defect obstruction to every fixed spectral R\'enyi order below one. These are source interfaces, not new unpaid assignments to rederive indefinitely. Their applications still require the entire expected description cost, including the reference normalizer and scale choice. The supplement is pinned at open-problems commit \texttt{bfb1d10b25cb5cb2e09112357cd24b21b5b1fe5e}; its mapping, seed, and campaign files were read directly during reconciliation. Publication of the threshold paper is already complete at public commit \texttt{cd27aa96a5bb670a5b3ae5451095b7020db8e9d4}; a stale upload instruction must not consume future mathematical runs.

\section*{Preparation note}
This report was prepared during recovery of the interrupted 11 October 2026 FEI work using OpenAI's GPT-6 Astra Pro with collaborating agents. It combines source-grounded analysis of the recovered manuscripts, accessible Brain notes, primary literature, independent mathematical audits, and explicit proposed research tasks. The A001--A100 assignments are a deployment design; they are not a claim that one hundred external Codex agents were launched. The scheduler verification record separately identifies the twenty existing tasks actually reconfigured.

\begin{center}
{\Large\bfseries\color{ink} A 100-agent research plan for the full\\[3pt] Fourier entropy--influence conjecture}\par
\vspace{8pt}
{\large Prepared for Dr. Yitzchak Shmalo}\par
\vspace{4pt}
11 October 2026; operational review completed the same day\par
\textbf{Campaign window: 11 October, 22:15:57 -- 21 October, 22:15:57}\par
Asia/Jerusalem (UTC+3 throughout this interval)
\end{center}
\vspace{6pt}
\textbf{Purpose.} Concentrate a hypothetical capacity of 100 Codex research agents on the missing mathematical estimates needed for unrestricted FEI, using the recovered threshold paper, the separate reductions manuscript, and the existing Brain obstruction archive. The same twenty research streams are assigned to the twenty existing scheduled tasks in this environment. This report is a private research plan; it is not part of the public paper.

\textbf{Current mathematical status.} The recovered preprint proves structured FEI results, led by an all-threshold theorem with constant below $8.937$. The joint witness construction and exact selector identities are valid tools. A universal bound for their remaining entropy cost is unproved. Neither this report nor the computations establish full FEI. A hundred agents can substantially expand the search and review capacity; the number of agents does not supply the missing inequality or guarantee a solution within ten days.

\textbf{Recommendation.} Spend the largest share of proof effort on the \emph{posterior cost of an entire Fourier-set witness} and on \emph{nonlocal restriction accounting}. Keep a substantial independent effort on signed Booleanity and product-biased gate inequalities. Use targeted falsification continuously, particularly on addressing, rare activation, near-tensor surgery, and actual Boolean realizations of proposed spectral laws. Treat every surviving universal reformulation as an unpaid obligation until its quantitative bound is proved.

\newpage
\section{The exact target and the starting assets}

For $f:\bits^n\to\bits$ under the uniform measure, set
\[
 P_f(S)=\wh f(S)^2,\qquad
 \HH(f)=\sum_S P_f(S)\log_2\frac1{P_f(S)},\qquad
 \I(f)=\sum_S |S|P_f(S).
\]

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\verification\concurrent_revision_a1ff935\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:54
 \qquad \HH(f)\le C\I(f).
\end{equation}
The constant must be independent of dimension, arity, depth, overlap, probability of the minority output, and any unbounded representation parameter. An explicit large constant would settle the conjecture. Optimizing that constant is secondary to proving its finiteness.

Throughout this report, entropy is in bits. Some historical Brain notes use natural logarithms and raw entropy of indicators. A coefficient in such a note cannot be copied into a sign-function statement without the corresponding conversion. Every imported result must state its measure, Fourier basis, normalization, log base, and treatment of the empty coefficient.

\subsection{Recovered manuscripts and verified scope}

The source recovered from the interrupted work contains a 36-page version of \emph{Fourier entropy of threshold functions and their compositions}, dated 11 October 2026 \cite{Threshold}. Its principal constant is
\[
 \Creg=\log_2(4+e)+2\log_2(e\pi)
       =8.93647465787758\ldots.
\]
With $D(f)=\sum_i\min\{\I_i(f),1-\I_i(f)\}$, its Theorem A gives $\HH(f)\le\Creg D(f)$ for every regular Boolean function. Here regular means unate with a total desirability order, and therefore includes every deterministic linear threshold function with arbitrary real weights. It does not mean small or uniformly comparable weights.

The proof proceeds through pivotal antichains, a relative resampling estimate, harmonic control of spectral ranks, and an Euler-product reference on Fourier levels. The proof was reconstructed in an independent audit during recovery; the product-biased threshold and bounded-overlap arguments were also independently read. No gap was identified in those audits. These are mathematical checks of a preprint, not external peer review or proof-assistant certification.

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\verification\concurrent_revision_a1ff935\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:65
       =8.93647465787758\ldots.
\]
With $D(f)=\sum_i\min\{\I_i(f),1-\I_i(f)\}$, its Theorem A gives $\HH(f)\le\Creg D(f)$ for every regular Boolean function. Here regular means unate with a total desirability order, and therefore includes every deterministic linear threshold function with arbitrary real weights. It does not mean small or uniformly comparable weights.

The proof proceeds through pivotal antichains, a relative resampling estimate, harmonic control of spectral ranks, and an Euler-product reference on Fourier levels. The proof was reconstructed in an independent audit during recovery; the product-biased threshold and bounded-overlap arguments were also independently read. No gap was identified in those audits. These are mathematical checks of a preprint, not external peer review or proof-assistant certification.

The package also contains logarithmic FEI bounds for Boolean combinations of regular functions and for read-$k$ unions of structured gates, and an arity-independent corrected FEI+ estimate for symmetric thresholds under every product measure. The latter composes through read-once threshold/parity formulas. The proof of a product-biased estimate for \emph{arbitrary weighted thresholds} is not supplied.

The recovery pass additionally brings the exact shared-data multiplexer identity from the separate reductions manuscript into the public paper, with the improved regular-leaf constant. If adjacent branches are orthogonal and selectors are fresh at each node, recursive multiplexers preserve $\Creg$ even when the branch data variables overlap. This is a concrete further class theorem, with explicit hypotheses.

The second recovered manuscript, \emph{Restrictions and witness coding for the Fourier entropy--influence problem} \cite{Reductions}, contains conditioned posterior forests, exact physical-restriction identities, and simultaneous hardness-preserving transformations. It supplies research targets and counterexamples for this plan. It is not silently substituted for the threshold paper or published as part of the report.

\subsection{Why the literature check matters}

The corrected Chakraborty--Kulkarni--Lokam--Saurabh work bounds LTF entropy by $O(\sqrt n)$; an earlier all-LTF claim was removed. The random-LTF result treats specified random models \cite{CKLS,RandomLTF}. This makes verification of the recovered deterministic theorem particularly important. The current argument must stand on its complete proof, independently of earlier abstracts or claims.


~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\verification\concurrent_revision_a1ff935\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:71
The package also contains logarithmic FEI bounds for Boolean combinations of regular functions and for read-$k$ unions of structured gates, and an arity-independent corrected FEI+ estimate for symmetric thresholds under every product measure. The latter composes through read-once threshold/parity formulas. The proof of a product-biased estimate for \emph{arbitrary weighted thresholds} is not supplied.

The recovery pass additionally brings the exact shared-data multiplexer identity from the separate reductions manuscript into the public paper, with the improved regular-leaf constant. If adjacent branches are orthogonal and selectors are fresh at each node, recursive multiplexers preserve $\Creg$ even when the branch data variables overlap. This is a concrete further class theorem, with explicit hypotheses.

The second recovered manuscript, \emph{Restrictions and witness coding for the Fourier entropy--influence problem} \cite{Reductions}, contains conditioned posterior forests, exact physical-restriction identities, and simultaneous hardness-preserving transformations. It supplies research targets and counterexamples for this plan. It is not silently substituted for the threshold paper or published as part of the report.

\subsection{Why the literature check matters}

The corrected Chakraborty--Kulkarni--Lokam--Saurabh work bounds LTF entropy by $O(\sqrt n)$; an earlier all-LTF claim was removed. The random-LTF result treats specified random models \cite{CKLS,RandomLTF}. This makes verification of the recovered deterministic theorem particularly important. The current argument must stand on its complete proof, independently of earlier abstracts or claims.

Existing coding and composition theorems are essential prerequisites, especially Wan--Wright--Wu and O'Donnell--Tan \cite{WWW,ODT}. Shalev's low-influence reductions explain why rarity alone is not a structural simplification \cite{Shalev}. Recent restriction work supplies a directly relevant stopping-tree framework \cite{GMP}; the plateaued-function theorem supplies a useful structured benchmark \cite{Potapov}. The author's percolation result supplies a geometric encoding example, with spatial hypotheses that must remain explicit \cite{Percolation}.

Do not spend the campaign rediscovering the generic coordinate-marginal entropy estimate as a Boolean breakthrough. Keller--Mossel--Schlank already give the relevant general estimate \cite{KMS}. A later preprint claiming an information-theoretic sharpening was withdrawn because the result already appeared there \cite{Withdrawn}. A new universal argument must use additional structure of actual Boolean spectra or a genuinely stronger global accounting mechanism.

\section{The two highest-priority mathematical endpoints}

\subsection{Endpoint J: bound a complete witness, including its posterior entropy}

For a nonconstant Boolean function represented as a union of gates, the recovered queue construction generates a subprobability law on Fourier sets through finite successful runs. A partition forest assigns every coordinate of a nonempty set $A$ to a gate, visits each used gate once, and describes gate subsets, roots, attachments, and child labels. Its probability is an actual probability of the fixed random generator; failed or infinite runs contribute no mass. A Fourier-set-dependent choice of a \emph{witness} is legitimate because the reference generator itself remains fixed.

\section{The two highest-priority mathematical endpoints}

\subsection{Endpoint J: bound a complete witness, including its posterior entropy}

For a nonconstant Boolean function represented as a union of gates, the recovered queue construction generates a subprobability law on Fourier sets through finite successful runs. A partition forest assigns every coordinate of a nonempty set $A$ to a gate, visits each used gate once, and describes gate subsets, roots, attachments, and child labels. Its probability is an actual probability of the fixed random generator; failed or infinite runs contribute no mass. A Fourier-set-dependent choice of a \emph{witness} is legitimate because the reference generator itself remains fixed.

The conditioned version chooses global positive weights $\lambda_l$ satisfying
\[
 \sum_{l:j\in R_l}\lambda_l\le1\quad\text{for every coordinate }j.
\]

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\verification\concurrent_revision_a1ff935\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:149
 \I(f)=\I_i(f)+\tfrac12\bigl[\I(f_i^+)+\I(f_i^-)\bigr].
\]
Thus a theorem asserting that every nonconstant Boolean $f$ has a relevant coordinate with $B_i(f)\le K\I_i(f)$ would settle FEI with constant $K+2/\ln2$. Conversely, a global FEI constant $C$ implies existence of such a coordinate with $K=C$, because $\sum_i B_i(f)\le\HH(f)$. This is an exact existential route to finiteness; it does not assert the inequality for every coordinate.

The scalar decrement bound behaves as
\[
 \kappa(u)=u\log_2(1/u)+\frac{3u}{\ln2}+O(u^2)
 \quad(u\downarrow0).
\]
Consequently a local influence budget can lose a logarithm. The task is to select a good coordinate, use a whole block, or amortize difficult splits against later credit. A finite restriction tree satisfies
\begin{align*}
 \HH(f)&=\E\sum_{v\text{ visited}}\Delta_{i(v)}\HH(f_v)
                +\E\HH(f_{\rm leaf}),\\
 \I(f)&=\E\sum_{v\text{ visited}}\I_{i(v)}(f_v)
                +\E\I(f_{\rm leaf}).
\end{align*}

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\verification\concurrent_revision_a1ff935\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:163
                +\E\I(f_{\rm leaf}).
\end{align*}
A successful stopping-tree theorem must bound the \emph{entire expected path cost} and the terminal entropy. The recovered threshold and formula classes provide useful terminal classes. The structural theorem guaranteeing cheap access to those terminals remains to be proved.

\section{Other routes that deserve sustained effort}

\subsection{Boolean phase and conditional entropy}

For an actual Boolean sign, $f^2=1$ yields the signed convolution identities
\[
 \sum_S\wh f(S)\wh f(S\mathbin\triangle U)=1_{\{U=\varnothing\}}.
\]
An arbitrary probability law on subsets need not admit signs satisfying these identities. The Brain's cyclic-label construction disproves a proposed dimension-free signed-lifetime bound for an auxiliary subset law, but that divergent law is provably non-Boolean \cite{Brain51}. It is a powerful warning against overbroad relaxations, not an FEI counterexample.

The reviewed phase-energy estimate for genuine Boolean functions with $X$-degree at most $d$, expressed here in bits, is
\[

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\verification\concurrent_revision_a1ff935\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:183
 H(P_f)=H(p_X)+H(\mathcal S_Y\mid\mathcal S_X),
\]
and the conditional term remains unpaid. The Brain also records an embedding showing that an unrestricted conditional-completion theorem even for one fixed cyclic marginal is FEI-equivalent \cite{Brain54}. A new result here must prove an effective structural hypothesis or a quantitative conditional estimate; it cannot merely invoke the marginal payment twice.

\subsection{Arbitrary biased weighted thresholds}

The public paper supplies the correct product-measure strong entropy normalization and proves it for symmetric thresholds. Extending this to arbitrary weighted thresholds with a constant uniform in weights, biases, and arity would immediately enlarge the depth-independent formula class through the established composition principle \cite{ODT,Threshold}. This is a substantial secondary target even if full FEI remains open.

The technical obstacles should be localized: the symmetric proof uses integer-valued sums, exponential tilting, a mode estimate, and a near-dictator regime. Weighted sums can lose the same lattice structure. The team should either establish a substitute for these exact uses or find a new proof. Testing a grid of biases cannot certify the endpoint or continuum statement.

\subsection{Shared-data composition}

For a selector $x\in\bits^a$ and arbitrary Boolean branches $g_b$ on the same data cube, let $F(x,y)=g_x(y)$. With $N=2^a$, set
\[
 R_T=N^{-1}\sum_b\wh g_b(T)^2,\qquad
 r_T(b)=\frac{\wh g_b(T)^2}{NR_T}.

~\mop_fei100\reports\fei-2026-10-11\verification\concurrent_revision_a1ff935\FEI_100_agent_research_plan.tex:230
